# Autonomous Cross-Sector Research QA Campaign — 2026-11

Campaign window: 2026-11-01 (first runs, fixes, reviews, merges), 2026-11-02 (new companies, reruns after the Tavily daily reset, further fixes) and 2026-11-03 (live reruns of the last fixes, three more fixes). Report written 2026-11-02 and finalised 2026-11-03 with the final rerun results (PYPL3, CLLS3, CLLS4, CLLS5; PRs #315-#317). The spec's hypotheses A–J, 15-dimension rubric, claim verdicts and gap taxonomy are used verbatim. Machine-readable twin: [`autonomous-cross-sector-research-qa-2026-11.json`](autonomous-cross-sector-research-qa-2026-11.json) (same facts).

Status rule used throughout: a defect counts as fixed only when a live rerun shows it gone. A merged fix proven by replay or code only is 'fix merged, not proven live'; a defect that disappears without a targeted fix stays open.

All company and run identifiers below are platform UUIDs (8-character prefixes in tables). No secret values appear in this report.

---

## 1. Executive summary

**Verdict.** InvestingBuddy is not yet a trustworthy research system. 21 corrective PRs were merged and deployed in three days. They made the deterministic financial snapshot much safer: it now withholds a number rather than publish a partial or mis-scoped one. They did not make the research useful. **After the fixes, overall usefulness at each company's latest run is still 3–4 out of 10 for seven of the eight companies** (HON 6, CFR 4, ADBE 4, ANTO 4, LIN 4, JPM 3, PYPL 3, Cellectis 3). Do not resume broad manual owner testing until the three CRITICAL items in section 17 have landed.

- **Panel.** 8 companies across 6 venues and 7 sectors, 17 scored reports: HON, ANTO and LIN before and after a rerun; PayPal on three runs and Cellectis on five; CFR, ADBE and JPM once (not rerun: budget and time). 16 of the 17 were claim-audited; HON after was scored but not claim-audited.
- **Claims.** 323 claims independently verified against filings and issuer documents: 150 PASS (46%), 89 (28%) STALE / WRONG_PERIOD / WRONG_SCOPE / WRONG_ENTITY, 23 WRONG, 13 UNSUPPORTED.
- **Gaps.** 114 of 175 independently audited 'missing' facts (65%) were DOCUMENT_ACQUIRED_EXTRACTION_FAILED: the platform held the document. Only 17 were genuinely not disclosed; 26 were not acquired (21 of them for non-US issuers); none was source-blocked.
- **Defects logged:** 168 (P0 9, P1 50, P2 81, P3 28). Fixed (a live rerun shows it gone) 13. Fix merged, not proven live 12 (offline replay only 7, not exercised live 4, not reproduced live with replay proof only 1). Open: partly fixed 19, absent on a rerun without a targeted fix 7, fix in an open PR 0, open 117. Final-rerun IDs (PYPL3-x, CLLS3-x to CLLS5-x) follow each evaluation's numbering, so a lineage re-logged on a later run is counted once per run (appendix).
- **P0s (9).** Fixed live: H1 (HON YoY sign), ANTO-1 (manufactured revenue conflict), CLLS-3 (chair 'no FY2024 results'), CLLS3-1 and CLLS4-1 (another SEC registrant's equities-exchange business filed as Cellectis's and leading the synthesis; introduced on CLLS3, relabelled but kept by #315 on CLLS4, dropped by #317 on CLLS5). Fix merged, not proven live (company not rerun): C1 (CFR x1000), JPM-1 (JPM total debt 7.7x too low). **Not reproduced live; fix proven by replay only:** PYPL2-1 (a takeover rumour stated as fact; on PYPL3 the claim was not regenerated and ADR-078 did not fire). Not reproduced, root cause unfixed: LIN-1 ('mines and sells' for a pre-revenue developer; the editor failed on the rerun).
- **P1s.** 40 of 50 are open, partly fixed or absent without a fix, and 5 more have a merged fix not proven live. None is an external blocker, so the campaign stop condition is **not met**. A new systemic P1, FW-1: the ADR-066 publisher firewall never ran in production before #315 (a swallowed AttributeError left it with no companies); it is live now but has not yet been seen acting.
- **Final reruns and day 3.** PYPL3 and CLLS3 ran on 48b5777 late on 2026-11-02. On 2026-11-03 #315 (ADR-079, and the ADR-066 firewall made live), #316 (ADR-076 amendment: the recency window anchors on a held disclosure; SEC chunks carry their filing date) and #317 (ADR-079 review) merged and deployed, and CLLS4 (a8f8f8a) and CLLS5 (6df9d89) ran. Cellectis reached its best score (mean 3.47, usefulness 3) after a P0 regression on CLLS3 and CLLS4. PayPal fell (mean 3.87 -> 3.67): the rumour vanished without the gate acting and the Q3 operating data regressed. **New top systemic defect:** forward target dates ('runway into H2 2028') are used as a document's period, and the period-agreement guard then discarded the only H1 2026 figure on CLLS5 (CLLS5-1, CLLS4-2).
- **Discovery.** Thesis fit on the materials thesis went from 0/15 to 12/15 after ADR-063 (live). Ranking was confirmed broken (score constant per source; the cap silently cut names: 6 of 7 theme-verified web names on E2, INFERRED from funnel counts because the cut names were never stored). ADR-070 and ADR-073 only partly fix it. Live on Discovery E3 b14b96d9: thesis fit held at 12/15, 3 of 7 evidenced web names were returned (E2: 1) and the cut is now named; but registry scores are still constant and a verified size still outranks fit, so adjacent large caps (SCCO, CLF, FCX) outrank Iluka and Meteoric, and Lynas and Lindian were cut. Recall (name-only leads) is the main open Discovery weakness.
- **Biggest generic weakness.** The platform acquires the right documents and does not read them, then writes a research state that is not derived from what it holds. Every one of the 16 claim-audited reports contradicts itself.
- **Deployed SHA at report time:** API 6df9d89 = main (GET /health commit_sha, checked 2026-11-03), web 95ba5f7 (x-ib-build-commit meta tag, checked 2026-11-03; nothing under apps/web changed since). Only Cellectis was rerun after #315-#317; no other company's score reflects them.

**Campaign conduct and cost control**

- The Tavily daily cap of 300 was respected on all three days. No provider limit was raised.
- No new paid provider was enabled, DeepSeek search stayed off, no source restriction was bypassed, no source access was purchased, and no browser or captcha workaround was added.
- No out-of-scope source project (SEDAR+ DDS, LSEG, browser automation, FCA FIRDS expansion, another search provider) was started.
- Disclosure: early on day 1 the owner's personal email address was sent in the SEC User-Agent header for a few direct sec.gov fetches. This was corrected the same day. The same class of slip recurred on day 3 (2026-11-03): an evaluation subagent sent 4 SEC requests with the owner's personal email address as the User-Agent before it switched to the project's research contact string. The address is not reproduced here.
- Deploy workflows have no concurrency guard. Rapid merges raced on day 1, and the db5baf8 smoke test failed and was superseded by the next deploy.
- Budget and time stopped four planned reruns: CFR2, HON3, JPM2 and ADBE2 were not run. Their fixes are verified by offline replay only, or by the same mechanism on another company.
- On 2026-11-03 only the Cellectis reruns CLLS4 and CLLS5 ran to prove #315-#317 live (56 Tavily credits). No other company was rerun after #315-#317; in particular LIN, whose iluka.com collision is the only one the #315 firewall replay found, was not rerun.

Dimensions: 1 Identity; 2 Primary-source quality; 3 Factual correctness; 4 Latest-period coverage; 5 Extraction completeness; 6 Financial semantics; 7 Business model; 8 Sector-specific; 9 Competitive position; 10 Catalysts / current; 11 Risks / counter-thesis; 12 Evidence consistency; 13 Entity separation; 14 Executive synthesis; 15 Overall usefulness.

Read the deltas bluntly. HON gained on the deterministic snapshot. ANTO gained from one entity-firewall fix. LIN gained on currency. PayPal is flat over three runs: on day 2 one new P0 cancelled one real improvement, and on PYPL3 the P0 vanished without the fix acting while the Q3 data regressed (3.87 -> 3.67). Cellectis gained on identity on day 2, fell on CLLS3 to a new P0 (another registrant's business as its own), and rose on CLLS4 and CLLS5 to its best mean (3.47) once the exchange text was dropped: entity separation 2 -> 5, business model 2 -> 4, synthesis 2 -> 4. Its latest-period score fell on CLLS5 (4 -> 3) and evidence consistency stayed at 1 on CLLS3, CLLS4 and CLLS5. Competitive position and sector-specific analysis barely moved: no report scores above 3 on competitive position or above 5 on sector-specific analysis.

**Score reasons**

- **HON (before, e50cb267)**: YoY sign wrong (-2.7% vs +7.9%), segment profit shown as group operating margin, pre-consolidation EPS beside post-split price, Q3 2026 10-Q acquired but not extracted, Honeywell Aerospace (separate registrant since 2026-06-29) catalysts/risks filed as HON, A&D playbook from stale SIC 3724, synthesis omits the spin-off.
- **HON (after, f0be3c4f)**: FIXED live: revenue +7.85% vs recast prior year (verified 37,442/34,717); net income -17.11% verified (4,729/5,705); Q3 2026 10-Q extracted (rev 4,994, NI 664); change-of-basis notice names the spin-off perimeter change AND the 1-for-2 consolidation; segment profit no longer shown as group operating margin. STILL WRONG: 2 HONA findings (Tempe plant; HONA standalone Q3 + $0.40 dividend) — H3 fix not yet deployed; backlog narrative mixes pre-spin $38.0bn/+12% with post-spin $18.8bn/+1% (new H10); 'newest disclosure is Q2' beside Q3 facts (H8); A&D playbook and A&D primes as peers (H4) for a post-spin automation company; chair cites an 'unresolved disagreement' about Q3 revenue that the facts do not support.
- **CFR (before, f722d620)**: Net cash/OCF shown x1000 (bn instead of m); Jewellery €5bn sentence validated as a group figure manufacturing a 'conflict'; a November-2025 H1 interim labelled 2026-Q1; Q1 FY27 sales present but not in synthesis; current developments are noise; AML/regulatory risk lifted from single secondary pages; peers = Swatch only.
- **ADBE (single run, 7b401826)**: The report identifies Adobe correctly and handles the non-calendar fiscal year correctly for the hard numbers. FY2025 (FYE 2025-11-28) and Q3 FY2026 (quarter ended 2026-08-28) figures from XBRL reconcile exactly, and the FY2025 YoY of 10.53% uses the right fiscal comparison. Beyond that snapshot the research is stale, generic or wrong. It presents the FY2025 three-segment structure as current, although Adobe merged into one reportable segment in Q1 FY2026. It treats the Sept-10 Q3 results as an upcoming 'checkpoint' and never shows Q3 YoY growth (+12.9%), ARR ($27.50B) or the raised FY2026 targets on the investor page. It misses the CEO succession (Chakravarthy, effective 2026-12-01), the CFO resignation, the Semrush acquisition, the DOJ settlement and $6.8B of 9M buybacks. Competitive analysis names Tempus AI as a closest peer, the peer table prints cash conversion as 140.69×, and F14 shows FY2026 revenue as reported before the year has closed. The research state contradicts itself: it says the newest filing is the 10-K, marks the latest-period headline question 'not acquired' while holding those figures, and labels 10-K figures 'press; filing figure differs'. Most of the 61 open gaps are disclosed in documents the platform already acquired but extracted 0 facts from. The root causes are no software playbook, a max_tasks stop, skipped 8-K disclosures and failed issuer-document extraction.
- **JPM (single run, 30828043)**: This JPM report gets the identity right, and most individual figures it states (FY2025 revenue/NI/EPS, Q2 2026 NII, deposits, uninsured deposits, capital rollforward, ratings) reconcile exactly to the 10-K, the Q2 10-Q and XBRL. As bank research it fails. Its headline 'Total debt' (64,776m) is short-term borrowings only, with long-term debt of 435,206m silently dropped. Its executive synthesis and chair verdict lead with an industrial misreading of negative operating cash flow and a -259% 'cash conversion'. It blocks on missing EBITDA/FCF. The bank metrics that matter (provisions/NCOs, NPAs, CET1 for 2025, SLR, LCR, ROTCE, TBVPS, overhead ratio, NIM, segment results) are all in the 10-K/10-Q the platform acquired and indexed, yet the report asserts they were 'not acquired'. It misses the Q3 2026 results released 2026-10-13 because it ingested a 2026-10-20 note-offering 8-K as its 'results release'. The Q3 10-Q is not yet filed (live EDGAR, 2026-11-01, latest 10-Q 2026-08-06). Catalysts and risks are filled with FASB effective dates, J.P. Morgan SE governance, a stale interchange trial and a 2023 First Republic page. Meanwhile the Apple Card portfolio acquisition and the $4.6B Visa gain behind the Q2 profit are absent from the V3 research. A subsidiary's ICAAP leaks into the executive synthesis as group capital adequacy. Overall usefulness is about 3/10, on par with Honeywell before fixes.
- **PYPL (run 1, f031b73b)**: The report gets PayPal's identity and every FY2025 XBRL figure and ratio right, and it correctly withholds total debt and keeps FCF and cash conversion. Beyond that it is thin and partly wrong. The latest Q3 2026 release (Item 2.02 EX-99.1) and the 10-Q were acquired and indexed, but the research state says the latest period's headline figures were 'not acquired'. Q3's direction (GAAP OI -8%, NI -14%), TPV, TM$, guidance and buybacks all go unreported, and stale Q2 product numbers from a chargeback blog stand in as the current picture. The legal picture is wrong. The 2022 class action is called 'resolved' though it is on appeal. The active 2026 Branded Checkout securities action is omitted. A gap claims no live regulatory action or litigation, despite FTC, CFPB, FCO and FCA proceedings in the 10-Q. Because the company is unclassified, no payments playbook ran: the synthesis frames customer balances as leverage, and the web follow-up searched for PayPal's 'nameplate capacity'. The CEO change, the buybacks that exceed FCF, and the €65bn BNPL forward-flow are missing. Usefulness is about where Honeywell stood before its fixes: right numbers, wrong or missing analysis.
- **PYPL (rerun, 39905de8)**: This rerun keeps every FY2025 and Q3 XBRL figure correct and fixes two before-run errors: the red team's misdated cash and the 'resolved' class-action misreading, though the second is fixed only because litigation now does not appear at all. ADR-076 is mechanically live. The headline-figures question now searches the corpus and runs a recency pass, which brought in issuer-sourced Q3 active accounts (440M) and transactions (6,796M). The Q3 economics a payments analyst needs are still absent although the release is held: TPV $487.1B, TM$, the GAAP OI -8% / NI -14% decline, FCF, debt of $13.5B, the $1.5B buyback and the guidance table. The research state contradicts itself: one finding names the July release as the latest issuer disclosure, and a gap says no Q3 10-Q or press release is in evidence, beside findings that cite both. The worst new defect is a P0: the executive synthesis presents a Stripe/Advent $53B takeover offer 'rejected by the board' as fact, on a single unclassified web page that no filing supports. Litigation and regulation are falsely reported as absent, the chair dates Q2 third-party product growth to Q3, and the company remains unclassified, so no payments playbook ran. Net usefulness is unchanged at about 3: one important improvement is offset by a new fabricated-looking catalyst at the top of the report.
- **PYPL (rerun 2 (PYPL3), 3992aa1d)**: This rerun no longer states a takeover anywhere. The Stripe/Advent '$53B offer rejected' finding was superseded (delta changed_facts), and no synthesis sentence, section lead, chair sentence or key point mentions an offer, bid or rejection. That happened because the claim was not regenerated, not because the ADR-078 gate fired: the editor rejected nothing for material_event_unvetted_source and the chair did not fall back. Every FY2025 and Q3 XBRL figure is still correct, and the chair now dates the third-party product growth to Q2, which fixes PYPL2-5. But the report is less current than PYPL2: the issuer's Q3 operating data (440M accounts, 6,796M transactions) is lost, the chair calls FY2025 and Q2 2026 the snapshot, and nothing from the held Q3 release's headline or guidance tables is used. The research state now actively denies holding issuer documents: F10 says the only revenue figure is third-party, F16 says the only dated source is a 19-Oct web page, two gaps deny Q3 primary figures and any issuer release, and the chair repeats 'no issuer press releases or filings ... or forward-looking guidance' as a key point. Litigation and regulation are again reported as absent against 10-Q Note 13. Buybacks larger than FCF, the Q3 profit decline, the debt rise and the CEO change are missing. Net usefulness stays at about 3: the P0 is gone, but latest-period coverage regressed and evidence consistency got worse.
- **ANTO (run 1, a68e6d31)**: The report identifies Antofagasta correctly. The Antofagasta-specific numbers it does state, mostly FY2024 revenue bridge items, net debt and capex, match the issuer's FY2024 results, and its Q3 2026 output and cash-cost findings are correct. But the financial core is two periods stale: FY2025 results and H1 2026 financials were never acquired, and the synthesis presents Dec-2024 net debt of $1.6bn as a 'manageable' current position when net debt was $3.97bn at 30 June 2026. A third of the ledger is other companies (South32, Lundin, Ecora). The risk section and the executive synthesis attribute Lundin Mining's Vicuña JV and stakeholder risks to Antofagasta, and the 'stable labour' claim is contradicted by the October 2026 Centinela strike. A manufactured conflict shows Antofagasta 2024 'revenue' as $156.6m (a volume variance) against South32's $8,296m. ADR-071 did not catch it, because each side states one amount and neither names a segment. The issuer's own IR pages are labelled 'reported in the press' and counted as zero issuer documents, so ADR-065's label fix does not reach non-US issuers without a sourced website. With no classification, no mining playbook ran: reserves, royalty, attributable basis, price sensitivity and the 2027 commissioning catalysts are all missing. Overall usefulness is below the pre-fix Honeywell baseline.
- **ANTO (rerun, c971490d)**: The rerun fixes the two worst defects in the before report. No other company's facts are filed as Antofagasta's (0 of 40, against 10 of 35 before): the ADR-074 guard dropped a South32 statement and recorded an entity_ambiguous gap. The manufactured revenue conflict is gone: the '$156.6m' volume variance now sits beside the $6,613.4m revenue with no conflict. Risks and the synthesis are now Antofagasta's own (the Centinela strike and the royalty law), and the current catalysts are right (2027 commissioning, 2027 guidance 630-690kt). But the financial core is still FY2024. Group FY2025 and H1 2026 revenue, EBITDA and net debt were not extracted, and the synthesis still calls net debt 'manageable' from Dec-2024 data when it was $3.97bn at June 2026. A new scope error labels Centinela's segment capex ($1,414.0m for 2024, $2,478.1m for 2025) as Group capex. Q3 2026 production and the cost-guidance revisions dropped out when the fused Q2/Q3 statement was discarded. Issuer IR pages are still labelled press or 'Unclassified', with 0 findings counted from issuer documents. The company is still unclassified, so no mining playbook ran. Overall it is a real improvement (2 to 4), still well short of a usable report.
- **CLLS (run 1, 1df9100c)**: The report researches Cellectis as a Euronext-only issuer (ALCLS·PA). It never links the Nasdaq ADS line (CLLS, 1:1) or CIK 1627281, so no 20-F or 6-K was read and the SEC path was skipped as 'not an SEC registrant'. Its 30 findings rest on old cellectis.com documents and trade press. The newest period is Q1 2024, ten quarters behind the Q2 2026 results filed on 2026-08-06. The historical figures it quotes are accurate against filings, but they are stale and several are labelled 'reported in the press' although they are filed interim financials. The single most material event, the 2026-09-14 exit from lasme-cel/eti-cel and pivot to in vivo .HEAL programs with a restructuring and an H2 2028 runway target, was ingested as a web document but never became a finding. The chair therefore describes a CAR-T company that no longer exists and asserts that 'no FY2024 results [are] available'. Biotech essentials are absent: cash ($169m), burn (~$21m per quarter), runway, pipeline stage, AZ's ~32%/40% control and convertible preferred dilution. The legacy council saw only EODHD price data and reads momentum as fundamentals. The entity firewall mostly holds (no other company's facts are filed as Cellectis's), and there is no ADS double-counting, but only because the ADS line is absent altogether. Overall the report would mislead a reader about strategy, pipeline and financial position.
- **CLLS (rerun, 686d71f6)**: The rerun fixes the identity-to-SEC link. ADR-075 resolves CIK 1627281 by exact name and France domicile, fetches the correct latest annual report (20-F FY2025, filed 2026-03-20, 1010 chunks) and classifies the company as Biotechnology from SIC 2836, so the biotech playbook applies. The false chair statements of the first run ('no FY2024 results', 'solid cash position') are gone, but only because the council refused and the chair fell back to a deterministic 'insufficient data' that miscounts the ledger as 0 findings. The 20-F is used mainly for risk-factor boilerplate. No statement figure was extracted, and the blocking cash_runway question and four others ran zero tools, although the 20-F in the corpus states $208.7m of cash and current financial assets at 2025-12-31. The interim period is still stale: the quarterly slot accepts only 10-Q, so the H1 2026 6-K (and its iXBRL 6-K/A) was never read. The newest figures are 9M 2025. The 2026-09-14 strategic transformation is still missed even though F18 lists that 6-K by date. The report still describes a CAR-T company and shows lasme-cel peak sales for a program Cellectis has exited. A new entity error files Calyxt's 2022 merger and BioFactory risks as Cellectis's counter-thesis, and 'Current developments' shows a boat builder, a Sanofi quote page and Vertex. No ADS-ratio or currency-conversion error was introduced, but the ADS and its 1:1 ratio are never shown. Overall the report is less wrong than before but still not useful for an investor.
- **CLLS (rerun 2 (CLLS3), df36d6f6)**: ADR-077 worked at the slot level. The quarterly slot now holds the correct H1 2026 interim report: the iXBRL 6-K/A 0001193125-26-389336, period 2026-06-30, 161 chunks, selected among 21 listed 6-Ks after the 20-F. current_period now names it. Nothing downstream used it. facts_read is 0, no H1 2026 revenue, cash, burn or runway appears, and the only cash figure is $246m at 31 March 2025, although the held 6-K/A gives $169m and runway into Q4 2027. The ADR-076 recency pass ran on three questions, but it anchored on a Sanofi quote page dated the run day, so its window (from 2026-09-18) excluded both the 6-K/A and the 2026-09-14 pivot. The core-filing chunks are undated in any case. A new P0 regression replaced the correct Therapeutics-segment finding: the Business model section and the first two sentences of the executive synthesis describe a U.S. equities/options exchange (Cboe EDGA, per the run's own gap) as Cellectis, labelled 'issuer filing' at 0.9 confidence. The Calyxt risk-factor error of CLLS2 is gone, and the editor ran this time. But the chair still reports '0 finding(s)' against 37 and now also claims 'No model was available'. Blocking cash_runway still ran zero tools, the strategic transformation to in vivo .HEAL programs is still absent, and catalysts are stale (ALPHA3) or about other companies. Overall the report is less useful than CLLS2, because a reader is told the wrong business in the first sentence.
- **CLLS (rerun 3 (CLLS4), 55f96fc3)**: Two fixes worked, one did not, and the P0 is unchanged. The ADR-076 amendment works as designed. The recency window anchored on the newest held disclosure (the 2026-10-28 .HEAL 6-K, an issuer filing), ran 75 days from 2026-08-14, and pulled in both the H1 2026 6-K/A (F15/F16) and the 2026-09-14 pivot 6-K (F14/F17). 20-F chunks are now dated, and ADR-077's 6-K/A slot is finally read. But what came through is thin. The only H1 2026 figure is a net loss that narrowed for FX reasons while the operating loss doubled and revenue fell 60%. The pivot reaches findings as an exit statement plus accession numbers tagged with the wrong periods (2027-H2, 2028-H2), and there is no .HEAL program, no runway, no cash and no burn. Blocking cash_runway still ran zero tools. ADR-079 did not achieve its stated outcome. The Cboe-style exchange page is now labelled 'third party filing', but F1–F3 were written again this run as 'The company operates a single reportable segment: an electronic market…', still lead the Business model section and executive-synthesis sentence 1, statements_about_other_registrant is 0, and no entity_ambiguous gap exists. The ADR-066 publisher firewall caused no harm but met no case to act on. The chair still claims '0 finding(s) … No model was available', gaps still contradict findings, and current developments now include Dassault Aviation and a vocational-school page. The report is marginally more useful than CLLS3 because the pivot is in it, but a reader is still told the wrong business first.
- **CLLS (rerun 4 (CLLS5), 36d046bc)**: The P0 is fixed. ADR-079's review (#317) dropped the other registrant's statements (statements_about_other_registrant=2, an entity_ambiguous gap, and the CLLS4 F1–F3 invalidated in the delta), and the Business model section and synthesis sentence 1 now describe Cellectis's AZ collaboration model. Only gap residue of the exchange remains (a fee-type gap and a 'trading venue' gap, both mis-reconciled to F1). The ADR-076 anchor still holds (2026-10-28, window from 2026-08-14), and ADR-077 still selects and reads the H1 6-K/A. But the run regressed on the latest period. The H1 2026 net loss that CLLS4 stated is gone, apparently discarded by the period-agreement guard because forward dates (2028-H2, 2027-Q4) are still used as period tags. The 6-K/A now appears only as metadata, 'latest primary filing with period figures', with no figures. Cash is the Dec 2025 $211M from a web copy, while the held 6-K/A gives $169M at June 2026, and the runway (H2 2028) and the .HEAL programs are still absent. New errors: IBA's irradiation-equipment price presented as industry economics, AZ's '$245m invested' (a regression), a share bridge that omits 16m AZ shares, and a garbage scope key. The chair still reports 0 findings, the legacy council still says there are no primary filings, and gaps deny cash, ownership and share counts that findings or held filings state. The report is more useful than CLLS4 because it no longer describes the wrong company, but a reader still cannot learn the cash runway or what Cellectis is now developing.
- **LIN (run 1, 796b644d)**: The report gets Lindian's JORC reserve and resource, granted Malawi licences, 100% SARECO ownership, Carester offtake terms, Gerald termination and interest-only FY26 income right, from real ASX documents. Its central financial picture is stale. It presents A$108m cash (30 Jun) and 'a cash position sufficient to start operations', although the acquired Sep-2026 Appendix 5B shows A$45.1m after a A$62.8m one-quarter draw, and no runway is computed. The executive synthesis wrongly says Lindian already 'mines and sells' concentrate, when first concentrate is only targeted for December 2026. Entity separation is weak: two of Lindian's own AR statements are attributed to Iluka, seven Iluka findings answer Lindian's questions, and Iluka is called the largest non-China producer. The research state contradicts itself: the newest disclosure is said to be the AR and 'no quarterly report is present'; gaps deny offtakes and ownership that findings state; the memo says no issuer document was found; and the chair invents a revenue disagreement. The most material 2026 jurisdiction risk is absent: Malawi's raw-mineral export order, the draft beneficiation regulations, and the August suspension and parliamentary scrutiny. So are dilution (+59% shares) and the ~45% share-price fall since the April placement. Usefulness 3/10.
- **LIN (rerun, 6d4f1c43)**: The rerun on 95ba5f7 is better than 796b644d on currency. It correctly names the 26-Oct Sep Quarterly and Appendix 5B as the newest disclosure, states 30-Sep cash of A$45,087k (from A$107,936k) and the quarter's A$24m project spend and A$28.8m SARECO payment, and gives the November energisation and December first-concentrate targets. The 'mines and sells' error is gone, but only because the editor failed (LLMJsonError) and the executive synthesis fell back to a first-finding-per-section list that says nothing about cash, timing or risk. The financial section is still wrong in substance: it presents koalagains' FY2025 figures (cash A$3.49m, equity A$58.4m, +0.98% shares) as the 'most recent fiscal year', computes no runway (5B 8.7 = 4.50 quarters, ~1.3 with capex), and a gap still says no post-placement cash exists. Entity separation is still broken: Lindian's own AR risk boilerplate is the counter-thesis, labelled 'Iluka's', and Iluka's zircon unit costs answer Lindian's unit-cost question without a name. Gaps still deny ownership, offtake volumes, cash and acquisition spend that findings state, and the V2 memo still says no issuer document exists. Malawi's export-order and beneficiation regime, the August suspension, Wei's retirement, the November meeting, dilution and the Stage 2 DFS are absent, and new tenement errors appeared. Usefulness 4/10 (was 3).

**Sector-specific questions (spec section 3)**

| Company | Run | Playbook applied | Captures the economics? | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| HON | both | industrial_defense (from stale SIC 3724) | No | Post-spin Honeywell is an automation company. The A&D playbook and peers (BAE, Thales, LMT) ask the wrong questions. Backlog is present but mixes perimeters (H10). |
| CFR | before | luxury | Partly | Region mix, DTC 77% and Jewellery/Watchmakers segment margins are present. Missing: price/mix, tourist/local demand, FX and gold sensitivity ('no research question assigned'). Peers are Swatch only. |
| ADBE | single | none ('no playbook declares Software & IT Services') | No | 4 of 8 software variables absent (ARR, retention, operating leverage, SBC) and 3 partial. Web queries asked about 'nameplate capacity'; gaps asked about mines and permits. ARR $27.50B, RPO $22.16B, SBC and the no-10%-customer statement are all in acquired filings. |
| JPM | single | banks_financials v1 (+ generic graph) | Partly, not enforced | The right core questions exist (capital, asset quality, NIM), but the generic cash-generation/EBITDA questions also ran and dominated the synthesis (fixed by ADR-069). Blocking questions were satisfied without their metrics (JPM-11). None of NIM, NCOs, NPAs, CET1 2025, ROTCE, overhead ratio, TBVPS or loan growth is in the report. |
| PYPL | run 1 | none (unclassified, SIC 7389) | No | Variables yes 0 / partial 5 / no 4 of 9. No. No playbook was applied (unclassified SIC 7389). The generic template asked about plants, mines, nameplate capacity and offtake, and none of the payments drivers (TPV, take rate, TM$, loss rate, active accounts, customer-balance interest, BNPL funding) was a required question. |
| PYPL | rerun | none (unclassified, SIC 7389) | No | Variables yes 2 / partial 4 / no 3 of 9. No. No playbook was applied (unclassified SIC 7389). The generic template still asks about permits, capacity and offtake, and none of TPV, take rate, TM$, loss rate or customer-balance interest is a required question. Active accounts and transactions arrived only because the recency pass happened to hit the ER operating-data table. |
| PYPL | rerun 2 (PYPL3) | none (unclassified, SIC 7389) | No | Variables yes 0 / partial 6 / no 3 of 9. No. No playbook was applied (unclassified SIC 7389). The generic template and the web follow-up ask about capacity, permits and offtake ('PayPal nameplate capacity ramp-up'). None of TPV, take rate, TM$, loss rate, customer-balance interest or BNPL funding is a required question, and the issuer operating table entered only as a Q2 column. |
| ANTO | run 1 | none (unclassified, sector=None) | No | Variables yes 0 / partial 8 / no 4 of 12. No playbook was applied (company unclassified, sector=None), so none of the economically decisive copper-miner variables were asked for: reserves, mine-level C1, attributable basis, royalty, price sensitivity, or the 2027 project ramp. |
| ANTO | rerun | none (unclassified) | No | Variables yes 1 / partial 8 / no 2 of 11. Still no playbook: the company is unclassified (sector=None) and the subject profile says 'commodities were not identified'. Reserves, mine-level economics, attributable basis, price sensitivity and the guidance trajectory were never asked for. The copper variables that do appear came from generic questions. |
| CLLS | run 1 | none (unclassified; SecExchangeNotSupportedError) | No | Variables yes 0 / partial 3 / no 5 of 8. No playbook was applied: the company is unclassified despite SIC 2836 on its SEC registrant. The generic and mining templates chased capacity, permits and offtake, and captured none of the biotech variables (runway, burn, pipeline stage, partnerships, dilution, AZ control). |
| CLLS | rerun | biotech v1 (SEC SIC 2836, via ADR-075) | In design only | Variables yes 0 / partial 2 / no 6 of 8. Partly in design, not in practice. The biotech playbook v1 now applies via SEC SIC 2836 and asks pipeline, R&D-intensity and dilution questions. But the blocking cash_runway question ran no tool, no 6-K is read, and mining follow-up templates still drive the web search, so runway, burn, pipeline stage and AZ control are all absent. |
| CLLS | rerun 2 (CLLS3) | biotech v1 (SEC SIC 2836) | In design only | Variables yes 0 / partial 3 / no 5 of 8. In design yes, in practice no. Biotech playbook v1 asks cash_runway and pipeline_state, both blocking, but cash_runway runs no tool, pipeline_state writes no finding from 16 items, mining follow-up templates still drive web search, and the held H1 2026 report is never read for figures. So runway, burn, pipeline stage and AZ control are all missing. |
| CLLS | rerun 3 (CLLS4) | biotech v1 (SEC SIC 2836) | In design only | Variables yes 0 / partial 3 / no 5 of 8. In design yes, in practice no. Biotech playbook v1 asks cash_runway and pipeline_state, both blocking, but cash_runway runs no tool, pipeline_state writes nothing from 16 items, the pivot 6-K is read only for its headline, and mining follow-up templates still drive web search. So runway, burn, current pipeline, trial timing and AZ control are all missing. |
| CLLS | rerun 4 (CLLS5) | biotech v1 (SEC SIC 2836) | In design only | Variables yes 0 / partial 5 / no 3 of 8. Better in design than in practice. Biotech playbook v1 now gets cash_runway answered, but from a stale web copy. pipeline_state is still blocking with 16 items and 0 findings, the pivot 6-K is read only for its exit sentence, mining follow-up templates still drive web search, and so the runway, current cash, burn, current pipeline, trial timing and AZ control are all missing or stale. |
| LIN | run 1 | mining_critical_materials | Partly | Variables yes 1 / partial 5 / no 4 of 10. Partly. mining_critical_materials drove the JORC, licence and offtake questions well. But it has no Appendix-5B runway, capex or dilution metrics, and it let a commodity-mention profile pull in aluminium. The binding economic variables for a funded-but-burning developer (cash vs remaining capex, first-production date, export policy) are not captured. |
| LIN | rerun | mining_critical_materials | Partly | Variables yes 4 / partial 4 / no 2 of 10. Partly. mining_critical_materials drives JORC, licence, offtake and timing questions, and ADR-076 now brings the 5B into the recency questions. But the playbook's cash, funding and balance-sheet questions are not recency-anchored and have no 5B runway, capex-to-complete or dilution metric, so the binding variable for a funded-but-burning developer (A$45m vs remaining spend) is still not in the Financial capacity section. A unit_costs question for a pre-production company invites a peer's costs. |

---

Every claim was checked against the strongest available source: SEC filings and XBRL companyfacts, exchange filings (RNS, ASX announcements and Appendix 5B), the issuer's results announcement or annual report, and EDGAR submissions. The InvestingBuddy output was never used as validation.

**Derived-metric audit (spec section 6)**

Calculations must fail closed when their inputs are incompatible. Before the fixes, several did not: the HON YoY sign, CFR x1000, JPM partial-sum total debt and the 7/19 double-counted totals all published a wrong number instead of withholding. After the fixes the deterministic layer withholds (PYPL total debt). The day-2 audit found the remaining failures upstream of it: a mine's capex labelled Group (ANTO2), a computable take rate and Q3 total debt reported as non-existent (PYPL), and no runway computed from a held Appendix 5B or 20-F (LIN, Cellectis). NOT_SHOWN marks a metric the report should have derived and did not.

| Company | Run | Metric | Report | Recomputed | Verdict | Checks |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HON | before/after | Revenue YoY FY2025 (before) | -2.74% | +7.85% (37,442 / restated 34,717) | WRONG | Prior year taken as originally filed (38,498, pre-Solstice) instead of the restated comparative in the same 10-K. Fixed ADR-062. |
| HON | before/after | Group operating margin FY2025 (before) | 21.71% (8,127) | bound 6,567 = revenue - COGS - SG&A - R&D | WRONG_SCOPE | OperatingIncomeLoss is total segment profit. Withheld by ADR-062. |
| HON | before/after | FCF FY2025 | 5,422 | 5,422 (6,408 - 986) | PASS | 10-K XBRL. |
| HON | before/after | Net debt FY2025 | 22,452 | 22,452 (34,939 - 12,487); ADR-067 total debt 34,580 | PARTIALLY_SUPPORTED | Arithmetic right on the FY2025 balance sheet, stale after the spin (Q3 2026 cash 5,694). Total debt carried a current-maturities double count before ADR-067. |
| HON | before/after | Revenue YoY FY2025 (after, f0be3c4f) | +7.85% | +7.85% | PASS | Verified against companyfacts. |
| HON | before/after | Net income YoY FY2025 (after) | -17.11% | -17.11% (4,729 / 5,705) | PASS | Verified against companyfacts. |
| CFR | before | Net cash / OCF FY2026 (Key Financials) | 8,496 bn / 4,880 bn EUR | 8,496m / 4,880m EUR | WRONG | x1000 unit error: the table unit was borrowed from the prose 'EUR 22.4 billion'. Fixed ADR-064 (offline replay on the real PDF). |
| CFR | before | Operating margin FY2026 | 20.0% | 20.0% (4,492 / 22,420) | PASS | Group. |
| CFR | before | Jewellery Maisons margin FY2026 | 30.5% vs 31.9% | 30.5% (5,037) vs 31.9% (4,896) | PASS | Segment scope labelled correctly. |
| ADBE | single run | Revenue YoY FY2025 | 10.53% | 10.53% | PASS | Correct fiscal periods, not calendarised. USD. Group. Both years are 52-week years. |
| ADBE | single run | Operating margin FY2025 | 36.63% | 36.63% | PASS | GAAP and group basis. |
| ADBE | single run | Net margin FY2025 | 30.00% | 29.997% | PASS | Rounding is fine. |

## 7. Factual failures

These are wrong values, unsupported claims presented as fact, and wrong entities shown as facts. The day-1 P0s were headline numbers from deterministic layers (XBRL period selection, table unit inference, debt-concept summation). They carried 'Regulatory filing' provenance, which made them the most dangerous failure mode. All three are fixed in that layer. The day-2 P0s came from the LLM layers that write prose on top of the ledger: a manufactured conflict (ANTO-1), a chair asserting that results do not exist (CLLS-3), a synthesis giving a pre-revenue developer a mining business (LIN-1), and a rumour stated as fact (PYPL2-1). Nothing in the current architecture verifies that kind of sentence. The last P0 (CLLS3, CLLS4) came from the evidence layer: another SEC registrant's exhibit, found by web search on sec.gov, was labelled issuer_filing from its host alone and became 'The company operates a single reportable segment: an electronic market...' at the top of the Cellectis report (CLLS3-1, CLLS4-1). It took two PRs (#315, #317) and two live reruns to remove.

## 8. Extraction failures

**This is the campaign's largest finding.** 'Missing' in a report usually means the platform held the document and did not read it, not that the issuer did not disclose the fact.

- The issuer-document fact channel read 0 facts for the 10-K/10-Q of HON (before), ADBE, JPM and PYPL, the Cellectis 20-F (the XBRL reader is us-gaap only, so IFRS is not read), Lindian's annual report and Appendix 5B, and Antofagasta's documents. US research rests on XBRL; non-US research rests on whatever an investigator happens to quote.
- JPM's gaps say no provision, NCO, NPA, CET1-2025, SLR, LCR or segment figure exists 'for any period'. All are in the 10-K/10-Q the platform indexed (1,663 + 735 chunks). The whole run made one corpus query.
- Cellectis's blocking cash_runway question ran 0 tools; the 20-F in the corpus states $208.7m of cash and current financial assets. Round 0 still ran tools=[] on CLLS3, CLLS4 and CLLS5; CLLS5 answers it from a web copy of the Dec 2025 release while the held H1 6-K/A gives June 2026.
- ADR-077 (#313) acquires Cellectis's H1 2026 iXBRL 6-K/A on CLLS3, CLLS4 and CLLS5 (live) and facts_read stays 0 on every run: no H1 figure on CLLS3, one net-loss line on CLLS4 and none on CLLS5, where a forward-date period tag discarded it (CLLS5-1).
- Lindian's Appendix 5B (26 chunks, state ready) holds the quarter's cash flow and item 8.7 (4.50 quarters of funding); no runway is computed.
- PayPal's Q3 EX-99.1 was read; TPV $487.1B, TM$, guidance, debt of $13.5B and the buyback never became findings, and a computable take rate (1.76%) is called 'only defined'. PYPL3 lost even the Q3 accounts and transactions PYPL2 had, and its gaps deny XBRL-tagged figures (PYPL3-2, PYPL3-7).
- ADR-076 (#312) makes recency questions read the newest held documents and adds the honest reason acquired_not_read. It is live on PYPL2 and LIN2, but covers recency questions only. Its amendment (#316) anchors the window on a held disclosure instead of a quote page and dates SEC chunks: live on CLLS4 and CLLS5.

Fix checks on E3:

| ADR | Verdict | Evidence | Gaps / failures |
|---|---|---|---|
| ADR-063 | PASS (holds) | discovery_intent themes [critical_materials, mining_materials], industries [Metals & Mining], size soft, end_markets [semiconductors, electrification, defence]; all 20 discovery queries are about critical minerals and mining (no defence-prime or semiconductor-equipment queries). The legacy top-level parse is still wrong (defense/semis/grid) but no longer drives scoring. | The intent turns 'critical OR specialty materials' into a hard industry constraint [critical_materials, mining_materials] and Metals & Mining only: no query targets specialty-materials producers (chemicals, high-purity materials, wafers/gases), and such a producer could never pass the industry constraint unless the registry miscalls it 'Mining' (Umicore). |
| ADR-070 | PARTIAL PASS | Within the web source, scores now vary: MEI/ILU/KNI 65 (theme verified, 45 pts) vs ABX/ATR 42.5 (theme_unverified=true, 22.5 pts), and every value recomputes exactly with score_thesis_relevance on the intent. Registry items now score on the intent too (platform 75, was 30). Basis is visible in relevance_reason ('score basis: matches theme ...' / 'searched for theme ... (not yet verified; half weight)') plus the theme_unverified flag. | Scores are still constant within each registry source (85 x6 curated, 75 x4 platform); Curated items in items[] show no 'score basis' clause, and no numeric breakdown is stored anywhere; For the admitted MEI/ILU/KNI the relevance_reason begins 'no verified statement of what the company does names the requested theme', which contradicts A3 verified_issuer (2-3 passages) |
| ADR-073 | PARTIAL | The cut is no longer silent: warnings[0] names 8 tickers (AU:LIN, AU:LYC, US:CRML, TO:ARA, US:NB, US:PKX, US:AAPL, US:GEV), and 3/7 admitted names are returned (E2: 1/7). The relevance tie-break works within a tier: MEI/ILU/KNI (65) rank ahead of ABX/ATR (42.5). | ranking_key = (eligibility, -hard_passes, -soft_passes, -relevance): a verified size (soft pass) outranks thesis fit, so 4 theme-verified admitted names (65, size unknown) were cut while the theme-UNVERIFIED ABX/ATR (42.5, size verified) were kept (deduced from the counts and the key); A3 theme verification never satisfies the industry hard constraint, so every web-admitted name stays eligible_unverified and ranks below all 10 registry names, including adjacent SCCO/CLF/FCX; funnel.beyond_cap is a count only; names live in a free-text warning (code UNCLASSIFIED) with no name, state, score or reason per cut candidate |

| E3-9 | P3 | Displayed score contradicts the rank; registry score gap is a label artefact | Ranks 1-2 score 75, ranks 3-7 score 85, ranks 8-9 score 75, rank 10 scores 85; curated 'Metals & Mining' earns +10 industry points over platform 'Mining' | D10 |
| E3-10 | P3 | Market cap rendered as '$0.00bn' | KNI size basis 'market cap 6,554,732 AUD ≈ $0.00bn → micro-cap' | new |
| E3-11 | P3 | Umicore still classified 'Mining' | UMI industry 'Mining' from platform_company_registry | D11 |

Flags: Iluka (rank 12) and Meteoric (rank 11) rank below adjacent SCCO/CLF/FCX; Lynas (AU:LYC) and Lindian (AU:LIN), both returned in E2, are now beyond the cap, while the theme-UNVERIFIED ABX and ATR (42.5) are returned; 4 theme-verified web-admitted names are beyond the cap (deduced; most likely CRML Tanbreez, ARA Aclara heavy rare earths, NB NioCorp Elk Creek, PKX); MEI is the target of an announced Lynas acquisition (ASX, 2026-09-30); the run does not flag it.

Limits of this check: Only stored data was used, plus two ASX markitdigital GETs (MEI and KNI header and announcements) to check the takeover and the market cap. No discovery or research job was run. The mapping of which 4 beyond-cap names are admitted is a deduction, not stored data.

**What worked:** the geography filter (A), size exclusions with cited floats or caps (B, C), cross-source de-duplication, honest empty results (C), and listing verification against official directories. Lindian, used as company case 4, came from E2.

## 12. Defects fixed

A defect counts as fixed only when a live rerun of the same company shows it gone. A fix merged and deployed but verified by offline replay or code only is listed as 'fix merged, not proven live' and is not counted as fixed. Partly fixed defects and defects that disappeared on a rerun without a targeted fix are open; they are listed here for traceability and again in section 15.

## 13. PRs / SHAs

Every PR had an independent review, often 2–5 rounds. The reviews caught real defects that would otherwise have shipped: a BAC double count; an insurer over-reach that would have stripped UNH, MMC, AON and BRK of their metrics; a regex with catastrophic backtracking on the event loop in the EDGAR index parser (143 s on a 100 KB page, the gunicorn worker-timeout failure mode); a cubic-backtracking trailing-code regex; false split detections; false drops of a subject's own facts; stale holdings reported as 'held'; a clinical 'topline results' 6-K picked as an interim report; a None resolver that dropped the subject's own EDGAR pages (#315 D1); exchange quote pages that would still have anchored the recency window (#316 D1). No issuer-specific exception was added. One defect no review caught: the ADR-066 firewall's swallowed AttributeError (#301), found only on day 3 (FW-1).

| PR | Squash SHA | ADR | Change | Defects | Independent review | Verification | Deployed in |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| #297 | 1f38d38 | ADR-062 | SEC XBRL: restated prior year, latest quarter, segment profit, change of basis | H1, H2, H5, H7 | 2 documented rounds. Caught: a wrong baseline that produced false basis changes; a doubled MRNA cover share count read as a 2-for-1 split (its EPS would have been withheld); GE's 1-for-8 reverse split missed by cent rounding; an old-quarter 10-Q/A treated as the latest quarter. | LIVE (HON f0be3c4f): YoY +7.85%, Q3 2026 facts, change-of-basis notice. Replay on 19 filers: no other filer gained a withholding or basis change. HON3 not run. | 1b14000 |
| #296 | db5baf8 | ADR-063 | Discovery intent follows the thesis text; a security code is never a name | D1, D2, D3 | 1 round plus a security review. Caught: materials-class over-reach ('specialty chemicals and advanced materials'); a missing word boundary; ambiguous aliases (transmission, grid, lender); B2GOLD/E2OPEN/4IMPRINT stripped as codes; a trailing-code regex with cubic backtracking on long Unicode-space runs that could hold a worker for minutes. | LIVE for D2 (E2 d6f5d2c6: themes critical_materials + mining_materials; thesis fit 0/15 -> 12/15). D1/D3: offline replay only. | 1b14000 |
| #299 | a68fb71 | (none) | Discovery: a current filer's public float stays usable until its next 10-K (MAX_FLOAT_AGE_DAYS 548 -> 670) | D6 | Independent review (rounds not recorded in an ADR). | Code and test only. Discovery D was not rerun, so ROK/SYM/POWL size placement is not live-verified. | 1b14000 |
| #298 | c940772 | ADR-064 | A table's unit is printed in the table; narrow-gutter two-column pages (pipeline v20) | C1, C2 (root fix) | 2 documented rounds. Caught: one row's 'EUR 2.1 bn' could scale a table of millions; a bare '000' read as a unit; a page-declared unit overridden by cells; reconstructed tables bypassing the page check; 'EUR 1950 million' read as column years. | Offline replay on the real Richemont FY26 PDF (net cash 8,496m, OCF 4,880m). CFR not rerun. No scale error on ANTO/LIN runs, where it was barely exercised (facts_read=0). | 1b14000 |

## 14. Before/after rerun comparisons

These compare measured state only. More generated text is not counted as improvement.

**HON**

| Measure | Before (e50cb267, pre-campaign) | After (f0be3c4f, 1b14000) |
|---|---|---|
| Factual errors in validated claims (WRONG / WRONG_SCOPE / WRONG_PERIOD) | 3 (YoY sign, segment profit as group margin, pre-split EPS) | 0 of these three (all verified fixed live) |
| Wrong-entity findings (HONA) | 4 (Tempe x2, FCA settlement, HONA backlog) | 2 (F33 Tempe; F17 mixed statement) |
| Open gaps (loop.gaps_open) | 58 | 58 |
| Issuer-document facts read | 0 (10-K and 10-Q 'figures not yet extracted') | 10 from 6 documents; 1 revenue conflict withheld (mixes an aerospace trade article, a Q1 10-Q figure and the Q2 release under one '2026' key) |
| Current-period completeness | Q3 2026 10-Q acquired, not extracted | Q3 2026 revenue 4,994m, NI 664m, cash 5,694m, assets 53,098m (single quarter) |

| Overall QA score / 15-dim mean | 3 / 4.20 | 6 / 5.73 |

Verdict: CLLS2 -> CLLS5: the best Cellectis score (usefulness 2 -> 1 -> 2 -> 3; mean 3.07 -> 2.53 -> 2.87 -> 3.47). Gained: the subject's own business restored after a P0 that took two PRs (#315, #317) to remove; SEC chunks dated; the recency window anchored on a disclosure; the H1 6-K/A selected on every run. Not gained: any H1 2026 figure on the final run, current cash, the H2 2028 runway or the current pipeline. Evidence consistency is 1 on CLLS3, CLLS4 and CLLS5.

ADR-070 and ADR-073 do not reach the eligibility and soft-pass layers that decide the order.

## 16. Systemic patterns

| Category | Hyp. | Companies / runs | Sectors | Venues | Frequency | Severity | Generic root cause | Fixed? | Residual risk | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Period tagging treats forward target dates as the document's period | G, B | Cellectis (CLLS4, CLLS5); the tagger and the period-agreement guard serve every issuer | biotech (observed) | Nasdaq / SEC 6-K (observed) | 2 of 2 Cellectis runs after #316 let the 6-Ks through; on CLLS5 it discarded the only H1 2026 figure (P1 CLLS5-1) and labelled the 10-28 6-K an 'interim report for 2027-H2' (P1 CLLS4-2) | P1 | The period tagger takes the first half-year expression in an excerpt (a runway or readout target such as 'H2 2028') as the evidence period instead of the filing's report date; the period-agreement guard then discards any statement that cites a results filing together with an event filing. Related guard damage on ANTO2 (a fused Q2/Q3 statement discarded, Q3 output lost: ANTO-4, ANTO2-2). | No | HIGH: it discards exactly the figures the day-2 and day-3 fixes finally deliver | systemic |

**Isolated vs systemic.** Every category except the first and the last appeared in at least two companies, across at least two sectors and venues. The first (period tagging of forward dates) has been observed on one company in two runs; it is ranked systemic and first because the tagger and the period-agreement guard are issuer-agnostic and it now discards exactly the figures the day-2 and day-3 fixes deliver. No fix special-cases an issuer. Non-US issuers are hit hardest: all four lost primary financials, and none of them scored above 4 on usefulness.

**Hypothesis verdicts in detail**

| Hyp. | Area | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | Corporate actions / economic perimeter | CONFIRMED; FIXED FOR THE US XBRL SNAPSHOT ONLY | HON before: YoY sign wrong against the restated prior year, pre-consolidation EPS beside the post-consolidation price, no perimeter caveat (H1, H5). ADR-062 fixes the prior year, detects the recast and the 1-for-2 consolidation, and prints a change-of-basis note (live on f0be3c4f). Still open: narrative KPIs mix perimeters (H10 backlog); recast detection compares only revenue and EPS; a segment reorganisation is not detected (ADBE-1, three dissolved segments shown as current); a mine's capex is published as Group capex (ANTO2-1); attributable and 100% bases are mixed silently (ANTO-9); a 2023 deconsolidated subsidiary's perimeter leads the Cellectis synthesis and its risk factors become the counter-thesis (CLLS2-2; the #317 review traced that text to Cellectis's own older 20-F, so it is staleness rather than another entity); a share bridge omits the 16m AstraZeneca shares (CLLS5-3). |
| B | Financial taxonomy | CONFIRMED; FIXED ONLY IN THE DETERMINISTIC LAYER | Fixed in the deterministic snapshot: HON segment profit as group operating income and wrong YoY (ADR-062); CFR x1000 (ADR-064); JPM total debt = short-term borrowings only (ADR-067; 7 of 19 replayed filers double-counted); industrial cash conversion and D/E on a bank (ADR-069); a percentage printed as a multiple (#303); a change amount read as a level (ADR-074). Not fixed anywhere above that layer: the council and red team swap periods and scopes (JPM-6: H1 as Q2, Corporate segment as Firm; PYPL2-5: Q2 third-party product growth spliced into Q3); FY2025 margins sit beside Q3 growth (ADBE); customer balances read as leverage (PYPL-4); Dec-2025 comparatives keyed to Q3 2026 (PYPL2-14); 'manageable net debt' judged on long-term debt alone, the number ADR-067 withholds (PYPL3-5); a cash-flow row label used as a segment scope key (CLLS5-5). |
| C | Document acquired but fact not extracted | CONFIRMED; LARGEST SYSTEMIC DEFECT; PARTLY MITIGATED FOR RECENCY QUESTIONS ONLY | 114 of 175 independently audited 'missing' facts (65%) were DOCUMENT_ACQUIRED_EXTRACTION_FAILED: the platform held the document. The issuer-document fact channel read 0 facts for the 10-K/10-Q of HON (before), ADBE, JPM and PYPL, the 20-F of Cellectis (IFRS XBRL is not read), the annual report and Appendix 5B of Lindian, and Antofagasta's documents. The blocking cash_runway question ran 0 tools while the 20-F stated $208.7m of cash (CLLS2-4). ADR-062 closed the US latest-quarter slice via XBRL; ADR-076 makes recency questions read the newest held documents and adds the honest reason acquired_not_read (live on PYPL2 and LIN2), but only for recency questions. The final reruns confirm it: Cellectis's H1 2026 6-K/A was held on CLLS3, CLLS4 and CLLS5 (ADR-077 live) with facts_read=0 on every run and no H1 figure at all on CLLS5; PayPal's Q3 issuer operating data was lost again on PYPL3 (PYPL3-2), and gaps there deny XBRL-tagged figures (PYPL3-7). |
| D | Subject / counterparty firewall | CONFIRMED; WORSE THAN REPORTED ON DAY 2; WRONG-REGISTRANT SEC PAGES FIXED LIVE (#317); THE ADR-066 FIREWALL WAS INERT UNTIL #315; IMPLICIT SUBJECTS OPEN | Fixed live: another company's statement cited from its own site (South32, Lundin, Ecora: ANTO 10/35 -> 0/40, ADR-074); another SEC registrant's exhibit found by web search on sec.gov and labelled issuer_filing from the host alone (P0 CLLS3-1: a Cboe EDGA exhibit became 'The company operates a single reportable segment: an electronic market...' and led the Cellectis synthesis). #315 (ADR-079) only relabelled it, because the page was a CIK-less paper filing (P0 CLLS4-1); #317 drops a CIK-less EDGAR page that never names the subject, and CLLS5 shows it gone (statements_about_other_registrant=2, entity separation 2 -> 5). Fixed in code, replay only: a separately registered namesake (HONA; ADR-066). Found on 2026-11-03 while fixing CLLS3-1: the ADR-066 publisher firewall never ran in production. _other_companies called .execute on the ToolSession, which has no such method, and the swallowed AttributeError left it with an empty company list from #301 until #315 (FW-1, P1); its replay finds one collision (iluka.com, LIN/LIN2) and it has not yet acted in a live run. Open: a statement with an implicit subject (Iluka unit costs answer Lindian's question, LIN2-3); the issuer's own annual-report text attributed to its offtaker (LIN-3/LIN2-2); another company's equipment price as Cellectis industry economics (IBA, CLLS5-4, passed the live firewall because the page was not IBA's own); a former subsidiary's text from the issuer's own old 20-F (Calyxt, CLLS2-2, back on CLLS5); a regulated subsidiary's ICAAP as group capital adequacy (JPM-5); unrelated companies in Cellectis 'Current developments' on every run (CLLS2-6). 22 WRONG_ENTITY verdicts in 323 checked claims, 12 of them Cellectis. Root cause: scope lives in prose; scope_key is not set from the evidence entity. |
| E | Multi-listing identity | CONFIRMED ON THE RESEARCH PATH; SEC LINK AND 6-K ACQUISITION FIXED LIVE; INTERIM FIGURES AND THE SECURITY RECORD OPEN; NOT OBSERVED IN DISCOVERY | Cellectis run 1 was researched as a Euronext-only issuer: ADR-061's issuer-vs-security identity lived only in Discovery, the CLLS/CIK sibling was never linked, no 20-F or 6-K was read, and classification failed (CLLS-1). ADR-075 (#311) links the SEC line at research time by directory name plus domicile (live on CLLS2-CLLS5: CIK 1627281, 20-F FY2025). ADR-077 (#313) selects the H1 2026 iXBRL 6-K/A (161 chunks, among 21 listed 6-Ks) live on CLLS3, CLLS4 and CLLS5. Still open: facts_read=0 on every run (IFRS is not read), so the interim's figures reach the report once (one net-loss line on CLLS4) and not at all on CLLS5 (CLLS5-1); the company record shows one line with no ADS, ratio, ISIN or reporting currency (CLLS2-9; unchanged on CLLS5); the identity tile reads 'Reporting Currency - sourced - Not sourced' (CLLS4-6). No ADS double counting or currency mixing was observed on any of the five runs. In Discovery no issuer appeared twice; GGG/ETM de-duplicated only because the stale lead was rejected; SIX:CFR vs SEC CFR (Cullen/Frost) is handled by ADR-066. |
| F | Security-code extraction | CONFIRMED; WKN FAMILY FIXED IN CODE; OTHER TOKEN-AS-NAME CASES OPEN | WKN A0NFN3 read as BB Biotech's name (D3), fixed by ADR-063 (WKN, ISIN, CUSIP, SEDOL, Valor shapes; replay only, Discovery A not rerun). Still open: TSE codes sent to SEC as CIKs (D8); a ticker as the issuer name (TSLA); a GICS heading as the name (Cognex, Ouster); an index and ETFs extracted as companies. In company research, ISIN is a 'blocking' identity gap on US filers that never print one (PYPL-14), and tenement IDs are assigned to the wrong projects (LIN-10). |
| G | Currentness | CONFIRMED; US LATEST NUMBERS PARTLY FIXED AND REGRESSABLE; RECENCY ANCHOR FIXED LIVE; FORWARD DATES CORRUPT PERIOD TAGS; EVENTS AND NON-US INTERIM FIGURES OPEN | Fixed live: the US XBRL latest quarter (ADR-062, HON), the results 8-K read through EX-99.1 (ADR-068, PYPL), recency questions reading the newest held documents (ADR-076; LIN2 latest-period score 3 -> 6), and since #316 the recency window anchored on a held disclosure rather than a quote page, with SEC chunks dated (#316; CLLS4 and CLLS5: anchor 2026-10-28, the H1 6-K/A and the 2026-09-14 pivot reach findings). New on the final reruns: the period tagger reads forward TARGET dates as the document's period ('2028-H2' runway, '2027-H2' readout; the 10-28 6-K called an 'interim report for 2027-H2'; CLLS4-2), and the period-agreement guard then discarded the only H1 2026 figure, the net loss, citing periods ['2026-H1', '2028-H2'] (CLLS5-1, P1). PayPal regressed: PYPL3 lost the Q3 issuer operating data PYPL2 had (PYPL3-2; its window was anchored on the run day, before #316). Still open: Cellectis's .HEAL programmes and H2 2028 runway (pivot exit stated only from CLLS4 on, CLLS-2); Antofagasta's financial core two periods stale (ANTO-3, ANTO-7); Lindian's Financial capacity shows FY2025 aggregator figures (LIN2-1); PayPal's CEO change (PYPL-9, PYPL3-9) and Adobe's CEO succession, CFO exit, Semrush and DOJ settlement (ADBE-7); guidance stated as an actual (ADBE-3, fix shelved); Cellectis cash is Dec 2025 from a web copy while the held 6-K/A gives June 2026 (CLLS5-2); superseded_by_finding_id unused. |
| H | V2 / V3 coherence | CONFIRMED IN EVERY REPORT; OPEN | Every one of the 16 claim-audited reports contradicts itself. Examples: 'newest filing is the 10-K' while the 10-Q is cited (ADBE-5); 'latest disclosure is the July release' while Q3 is cited (PYPL2-3); 'most recent disclosure covers FY2025' while H1 and Q3 2026 are cited (ANTO-5); the V2 memo says no issuer document was found while 5 ASX documents are held (LIN-5); the legacy council says 'no company disclosures' while the ledger holds 17 issuer-filing findings (CLLS-4); the chair says 0 findings while the ledger holds 37, 37, 38 and 32 (CLLS2-3, on CLLS2-CLLS5); PYPL3's chair key point, two gaps, F10 and F16 deny holding any issuer release or filing beside the cited 10-Q and 8-K (PYPL3-1); the legacy council says 'no primary filings' on every Cellectis run (CLLS-4); gaps deny facts that findings state (ANTO2-3, CLLS2-8, LIN2-4); 61 gaps on the ADBE page vs 73 in the loop. |
| I | Council status | CONFIRMED; HEADER FIXED IN CODE; DEGRADED RUNS ARE COMMON AND SIGNALLED UNRELIABLY | HON before: header '7 agents completed' while 1 failed (H7); ADR-062 s.5 prints 'N of M agents completed, K failed' (not live-observed: no later run had a failed agent). Three of the four day-2 reruns were degraded: ANTO2 used the deterministic chair because no chair model was available (recorded in degraded); CLLS2's council refused on a blocking question and the fallback states '0 findings' with the wrong reason (CLLS2-3); the editor failed with LLMJsonError on LIN2 and CLLS2, and LIN2's executive synthesis became a first-finding-per-section list. All four final reruns (PYPL3, CLLS3, CLLS4, CLLS5) had a degraded element: CLLS3, CLLS4 and CLLS5 used the deterministic chair fallback, which states '0 finding(s) ... No model was available' although the chair slot is routed to azure_openai and the council actually refused on a blocking question (CLLS2-3); PYPL3's red team was withheld whole by the safety gate (council 7 of 8, PYPL3-13). Completeness also over-states: blocking questions 'satisfied' without their metric (JPM-11) or with 0 findings (PYPL2-12); 'Evidence: Strong' on a report with a P0 (HON before). |
| J | Discovery triage | CONFIRMED; PARTLY FIXED (LIVE ON E3) | relevance_score_pre_scan was constant per source in every stored run (B 90 x3; D 86/66; E 80.5/60.5; E2 85/30/20) and did not drive the order (E2 shows 30 above 85). Web items got 45 circular theme points or 0. The cap of 15 silently cut 8 E2 names, including 6 of 7 theme-verified web candidates (inferred from counts; names not persisted). ADR-070 scores dynamic items on the intent and half-weights unverified themes; ADR-073 applies relevance before the cap and records funnel.beyond_cap. Residual by design: relevance is the last sort key after eligibility tier and verified-pass counts, so a size-unknown mega cap can still outrank a better-fitting small cap (D5/D7). Live on E3 b14b96d9 (95ba5f7): web scores now vary (65 theme-verified vs 42.5 unverified) and each recomputes on the intent; 3 of 7 admitted web names returned (E2: 1 of 7); the cut is named, by ticker only. Registry scores are still constant (curated 85 x6, platform 75 x4); the score basis of admitted MEI, ILU and KNI says no verified statement names the theme, contradicting their A3 verification; a verified size outranks fit, so 4 admitted names were cut while theme-unverified ABX and ATR were returned (E3-2); theme verification never satisfies the industry constraint, so every web name ranks below all 10 registry names, including adjacent SCCO, CLF and FCX (E3-1). Thesis fit stayed 12/15. |

---

## 17. Recommended engineering priorities

### CRITICAL

**P-1. Period tagging: a forward target date is never the document's period**

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Problem | The period tagger takes the first half-year expression in an excerpt as the evidence period, so a release that says 'runway into H2 2028' or 'data in H2 2027' is filed as a 2028-H2 or 2027-H2 document. The period-agreement guard then discards statements that combine it with a results filing. |
| Concrete evidence | CLLS5-1 (P1): the only H1 2026 figure, the net loss that CLLS4 stated exactly, was discarded over periods ['2026-H1', '2028-H2']; the 10-28 6-K is called an 'interim report for period 2027-H2' and 6-K/A-period items are labelled 2027-Q4. CLLS4-2 (P1): the pivot and .HEAL 6-Ks reach findings only as metadata tagged 2027-H2 and 2028-H2. Related period-key defects: ANTO-10 (every 2025/2026 finding None), CLLS-7, PYPL2-14 (Dec-2025 comparatives keyed Q3 2026), PYPL3-14 (web findings with period_key None), C3; related guard damage ANTO-4/ANTO2-2. |
| Affected cases | Observed on Cellectis (CLLS4, CLLS5); the tagger and the guard serve every issuer whose releases name future dates (every biotech, every company that guides) |
| Expected benefit | Stops the platform from discarding the figures that ADR-076, ADR-077 and #316 now deliver; makes recency, supersession and the period-agreement guard trustworthy. |
| Rough scope | Small-medium: take the period from the filing's report date (EDGAR reportDate, the 6-K/A period_end, the results-release heading) and never from a target-date phrase; tag forward dates as targets, not periods; the period-agreement guard ignores target dates and event filings; period keys for web, IR and comparative-column findings (formerly P-11). |
| Dependency | None. |
| Before more owner testing? | YES |

**P-2. One research state derived from the ledger: counts, gaps and 'latest disclosure'**

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Problem | Gaps, 'latest disclosure', question status, chair counts and the V2 memo contradict the findings in the same report. |
| Concrete evidence | 16 of 16 claim-audited reports self-contradict. The Cellectis chair says '0 finding(s) ... No model was available' against 37, 37, 38 and 32 ledger findings on four consecutive runs (CLLS2-3); the legacy council says there are no primary filings on every Cellectis run (CLLS-4); PYPL3's chair key point, two gaps, F10 and F16 deny holding any issuer release or filing beside the cited 10-Q and 8-K (PYPL3-1); gaps deny facts that findings state on PayPal, Antofagasta, Cellectis and Lindian (PYPL3-7, ANTO2-3, CLLS2-8, LIN2-4); also PYPL2-3, ANTO-5, LIN-5, ADBE-5, H8, H11; questions 'satisfied' with 0 findings (PYPL2-12, PYPL3-10) or without their metric (JPM-11); 61 vs 73 ADBE gaps. |
| Affected cases | 8 of 8 companies |
| Expected benefit | The owner can trust what the page says the platform knows and does not know. Prerequisite for any completeness or readiness label. |
| Rough scope | Medium-large: compute newest document, latest period, segment structure and ledger counts once per run; derive gaps and question status from the ledger and inventory; make the chair fallback and the V2 memo read the same state. |
| Dependency | Benefits from P-3 and P-5; can start independently. |
| Before more owner testing? | YES |

**P-3. Non-US primary financial extraction: IFRS XBRL, 6-K interim figures, ASX Appendix 5B**

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Problem | Non-US filings are now acquired (the 20-F, the H1 6-K/A, the Appendix 5B) and none of their statement figures is extracted, so cash, burn, runway and the latest half-year reach the report only by chance through prose. |
| Concrete evidence | Cellectis: the H1 2026 iXBRL 6-K/A held on CLLS3, CLLS4 and CLLS5 with facts_read=0 on every run (the XBRL reader is us-gaap only); one net-loss line on CLLS4, none on CLLS5; cash is Dec 2025 from a web copy (CLLS5-2) and the 20-F's $208.7m was never read (CLLS2-4). Lindian: the Appendix 5B holds the quarter's cash flow and item 8.7 (4.50 quarters), no runway computed (LIN-2). Antofagasta's documents read 0 facts (ANTO-7). 21 of 26 DOCUMENT_NOT_ACQUIRED gaps and 62 of 114 DOCUMENT_ACQUIRED_EXTRACTION_FAILED gaps are on non-US runs. |
| Affected cases | 4 of 4 non-US companies (CFR, ANTO, CLLS, LIN) |
| Expected benefit | Current financials, runway and burn for dual-listed and non-US issuers; the biotech and mining playbooks can answer their blocking questions from held filings. |
| Rough scope | Large, in slices: (a) ifrs-full XBRL for 20-F filers and iXBRL 6-K/A interims (ADR-077 already selects them); (b) statement tables from non-iXBRL results 6-Ks; (c) ASX Appendix 5B and annual-report layouts; (d) UK RNS/IR results PDFs; (e) the financial questions fall back to the corpus when the fact tools return nothing. |
| Dependency | P-1 (or extracted interim figures will be discarded by mis-tagged periods). ADR-075 and ADR-077 merged and live. |
| Before more owner testing? | YES |

### HIGH

**P-4. A claim gate on the synthesis and the chair**

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Problem | The executive synthesis and the chair state things the ledger does not support: rumours as fact, a wrong business stage, spliced periods, invented counts or disagreements. The P0 class moved: no P0 on the final reruns came from this layer, but nothing yet stops it. |
| Concrete evidence | P0s PYPL2-1 (takeover rumour as fact), LIN-1 ('mines and sells' for a pre-revenue developer), CLLS-3 ('no FY2024 results'); P1s PYPL2-5 (Q2 figures in a Q3 statement), CLLS2-3, ANTO-5, LIN-6, H11. LIN-1 and CLLS-3 vanished on rerun only because the editor or chair fell back. Final reruns: ADR-078 has never fired in a live run (on PYPL3 the claim was simply not regenerated), and PYPL3-4 shows an engagement conclusion drawn from one third-party figure, outside ADR-078's event vocabulary. |
| Affected cases | 5 of 8 companies (HON, PYPL, ANTO, CLLS, LIN) |
| Expected benefit | Removes the most damaging P0 class: a confident sentence at the top of the report that is false. |
| Rough scope | Medium: claim-type gate for material corporate events (ADR-078, #314, merged); tense/stage check against the ledger's lifecycle facts; period labels carried into chair sentences; chair counts and disagreement ids validated against the ledger; 'not in evidence' never phrased as 'does not exist'. |
| Dependency | ADR-078 (#314, 48b5777, merged; replay only, not exercised live on PYPL3) covers the material-event part. |
| Before more owner testing? | YES |

**P-5. Read what was acquired for US filers; never call a held fact 'not acquired'**

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Problem | US filers' 10-K/10-Q and results EX-99.1 are acquired and indexed, but statement facts beyond the XBRL snapshot are not extracted, and gap writers assert absence from a per-question slice. |
| Concrete evidence | facts_read=0 for the 10-K/10-Q of HON (before), ADBE, JPM and PYPL; JPM bank KPIs 'not acquired' beside 2,398 indexed chunks (JPM-4); PYPL3 lost the Q3 issuer operating data PYPL2 had (PYPL3-2) and its gaps deny XBRL-tagged FY2024, Q3 2025 and interest-expense figures (PYPL3-7); 114 of 175 audited gaps (all companies) are DOCUMENT_ACQUIRED_EXTRACTION_FAILED. |
| Affected cases | 4 US companies (HON, ADBE, JPM, PYPL); the absence-gap check applies to all 8 |
| Expected benefit | Bank KPIs, TPV and take rate, guidance and buybacks reach US reports; false absence claims leave investor pages. |
| Rough scope | Large, in slices: (a) the gap writer checks the corpus, XBRL companyfacts and the document inventory before asserting absence, and uses acquired_not_read beyond recency questions; (b) statement-table extraction for 10-K/10-Q and the Item 2.02 EX-99.1. |
| Dependency | ADR-068 and ADR-076 (+ amendment) merged. Slice (a) has none. |
| Before more owner testing? | YES |
