---

## 18. Recommendation on product readiness

**NOT READY beyond private, owner-supervised use. Do NOT resume broad manual owner testing.**

- Overall usefulness at each company's latest run is 3-4/10 for seven of eight companies (HON 6; CFR, ADBE, ANTO and LIN 4; JPM, PYPL and Cellectis 3). The fixes made the structured snapshot safer and removed the worst entity error; they did not make the research useful.
- Of 9 P0s, 5 are fixed live (H1, ANTO-1, CLLS-3, CLLS3-1, CLLS4-1). C1 and JPM-1 are fix merged, not proven live (CFR and JPM never rerun). PYPL2-1 is not reproduced live and its fix is proven by replay only: on PYPL3 the claim was simply not regenerated and ADR-078 did not fire. LIN-1 vanished only because the editor failed; its root cause is untouched.
- The final reruns showed a fix and a regression in the same run: CLLS5 removed the P0 and lost the only H1 2026 figure to a forward-date period tag (CLLS5-1). PYPL3 lost the Q3 operating data PYPL2 had. Gains do not hold from one run to the next.
- A guard credited since day 1, the ADR-066 publisher firewall, did nothing in production until #315 on 2026-11-03 (FW-1) because of a swallowed AttributeError, and it has not yet been seen acting in a live run. 'Merged and deployed' is not evidence that a guard runs.
- The campaign stop condition is not met: 40 P1s are open, partly fixed or absent without a fix, and 5 more have a merged fix not proven live. None is an external blocker; they are open because of time and scope.
- Every one of the 16 claim-audited reports contradicts itself about what it holds, and labels overstate quality ('Evidence: Strong' on a report with a P0; 'complete analysis' with blocking questions unanswered; the Cellectis chair saying 0 findings on four runs). A human approver cannot catch these without independent checking, so human approval before publication remains necessary but is not sufficient.

- **Allowed now:** Targeted owner checks of what was fixed and rerun live: HON perimeter and EPS, the Antofagasta entity firewall, Lindian's Sep-quarter cash, the Cellectis SEC link and 6-K/A selection, the Cellectis wrong-registrant fix (CLLS5), Discovery materials intent. Not PayPal's Q3 operating data: it regressed on PYPL3.
- **Gate for broad owner testing:** P-1, P-2 and P-3 merged, deployed and rerun on at least HON, CFR, JPM, PYPL, Cellectis and Lindian; ADR-078 observed firing in a live run; the live ADR-066 firewall observed acting in a live run (LIN); P-10 (statement kind) at least designed.
- **Safety posture:** Unchanged: evidence-first, citation-bound, no public recommendations, human approval before every publication, admin-gated routes. The platform remains private-use; the UK NSM and ASX source terms still require consent before any public launch.

**Campaign stop condition (spec section 20)**

| Stop condition | Status |
|---|---|
| All eight company cases read and scored | MET: 8 of 8 scored; 5 of them rerun after fixes (HON, PYPL, ANTO, Cellectis, LIN), PayPal twice and Cellectis four times: 17 scored reports, 16 of them claim-audited (HON after was scored, not claim-audited). |
| All feasible Discovery cases inspected | MET: 7 runs inspected (A, B, C, D, E, E2, E3). |
| Important claims independently verified | MET: 323 claims across 16 claim-audited reports (at least 3 financial, 2 business, 2 current-event and 1 risk claim per report). |
| All P0/P1 fixed and rerun, or documented as an external blocker | NOT MET. P0: 5 of 9 fixed live; C1 and JPM-1 fix merged, not proven live; PYPL2-1 not reproduced live, fix proven by replay only; LIN-1 root cause unfixed. P1: 40 open, partly fixed or absent without a fix (among them CLLS5-1, CLLS4-2, CLLS2-3, CLLS2-4, PYPL3-1, PYPL3-2, PYPL3-3) and 5 with a merged fix not proven live (C2, ADBE-6, JPM-2, JPM-3, FW-1). None is an external blocker: every one is engineering work left open for time and scope. |
| Systemic P2 issues ranked | MET (section 17). |
| Final QA report exists | MET: this document and its JSON twin. |

The campaign is **not** complete under its own stop condition. This report is the final artifact of the campaign window, not a completion claim.

### Product judgment by hypothesis (A–J)

Unsoftened. Each line says whether the owner can rely on that area today.

**A. Corporate actions / economic perimeter: CONFIRMED; FIXED FOR THE US XBRL SNAPSHOT ONLY.** Do not trust perimeter handling outside the US XBRL headline numbers. The one case that was fixed (HON) was fixed because the 10-K tags a restated comparative. Anything narrative, segment-level, mine-level or non-US is unguarded, and the campaign found a new perimeter error (ANTO2-1) on day 2 and a share bridge that omits 16m AstraZeneca shares on the final Cellectis run (CLLS5-3).

**B. Financial taxonomy: CONFIRMED; FIXED ONLY IN THE DETERMINISTIC LAYER.** The structured snapshot now fails closed: it withholds rather than publishes a partial or mis-scoped aggregate. Every LLM-written layer (investigator, council, red team, chair, editor) still mixes periods and scopes, and no verifier stops it. 'Fixed' is true only for the deterministic layer.

**C. Document acquired but fact not extracted: CONFIRMED; LARGEST SYSTEMIC DEFECT; PARTLY MITIGATED FOR RECENCY QUESTIONS ONLY.** This is the product's central failure. 'Missing' in a report usually means 'held and not read', and the reader is told it was not acquired. Until statement extraction works for 10-K/10-Q/EX-99.1, 20-F/6-K (IFRS) and ASX/UK annual and quarterly documents, every report under-states what the platform knows and every completeness label is unreliable.

**D. Subject / counterparty firewall: CONFIRMED; WORSE THAN REPORTED ON DAY 2; WRONG-REGISTRANT SEC PAGES FIXED LIVE (#317); THE ADR-066 FIREWALL WAS INERT UNTIL #315; IMPLICIT SUBJECTS OPEN.** Less trustworthy than the day-2 report said. One guard credited since day 1 (the ADR-066 publisher firewall) did nothing in production until #315 on 2026-11-03, and no test caught it. The worst entity error of the campaign entered on CLLS3 through a door no guard watched (a sec.gov web page trusted for its host) and took two PRs and two live reruns to close. Each guard still covers one sentence shape: the run that removed the exchange admitted IBA's equipment price. A reader cannot assume a finding is about the subject, and a guard cannot be counted as working until a live run shows it acting.

**E. Multi-listing identity: CONFIRMED ON THE RESEARCH PATH; SEC LINK AND 6-K ACQUISITION FIXED LIVE; INTERIM FIGURES AND THE SECURITY RECORD OPEN; NOT OBSERVED IN DISCOVERY.** The platform now finds a dual-listed foreign filer's SEC filings and its latest interim, proven live. It still cannot read the interim's figures, and it shows the reader one listing. Treat any dual-listed non-US company as under-researched until an interim's statement figures reach the report and the security list is persisted.

**F. Security-code extraction: CONFIRMED; WKN FAMILY FIXED IN CODE; OTHER TOKEN-AS-NAME CASES OPEN.** The named example is fixed in code. The general rule (a code, ticker, heading or index is never a company) is not, and Discovery still loses real names to it. Low severity for research, real for Discovery recall.

**G. Currentness: CONFIRMED; US LATEST NUMBERS PARTLY FIXED AND REGRESSABLE; RECENCY ANCHOR FIXED LIVE; FORWARD DATES CORRUPT PERIOD TAGS; EVENTS AND NON-US INTERIM FIGURES OPEN.** The newest numbers reach US reports through XBRL, and PYPL3 shows that coverage can regress from one run to the next. For non-US issuers the platform now acquires the right interim and anchors recency on a real disclosure, proven live on Cellectis, and then a period tagger that reads 'runway into H2 2028' as the document's period throws the figure away. 'Latest' cannot be trusted for any non-US issuer, for anything that is not an XBRL figure, or for the period label of any finding drawn from a release that names a future date.

**H. V2 / V3 coherence: CONFIRMED IN EVERY REPORT; OPEN.** The investor page and the technical ledger do not describe the same research state, and nothing reconciles them. Gaps, 'latest disclosure' and question status are written per question from a partial evidence slice, not derived from the ledger and the document inventory.

**I. Council status: CONFIRMED; HEADER FIXED IN CODE; DEGRADED RUNS ARE COMMON AND SIGNALLED UNRELIABLY.** The UI can still imply a normal council run. Degraded states are recorded in report data, but one fallback misstates its own count and reason, and the evaluations did not confirm a page-level marker for editor fallbacks. A degraded run was the norm after day 1 (7 of 8 reruns), not the exception, and the Cellectis fallback has misstated its own count and reason on four consecutive runs.

**J. Discovery triage: CONFIRMED; PARTLY FIXED (LIVE ON E3).** As observed, ranking was meaningless: it measured the source, not the fit. After ADR-070 and ADR-073 it measures fit only inside the web source, and only after eligibility, hard passes and size verification. Live on E3 that is not enough: the best-evidenced small caps (Iluka 12th, Meteoric 11th; Lynas and Lindian cut) still sit below adjacent large caps, and a candidate under an announced takeover (Meteoric) is not flagged. Discovery triage is better than before and still not an order a reader can rely on.

### Spec section 19 questions

**A. Is company research useful today?**
No, not as research. It is a starting point that an analyst must redo. Final overall-usefulness scores: HON 6 (after fixes), CFR 4, ADBE 4, LIN 4, ANTO 4, JPM 3, PYPL 3, Cellectis 3. Seven of eight companies score 3-4 on usefulness at their latest run, after up to 21 merged corrective PRs. At the latest run the 15-dimension mean is 3.47-4.13 for six of eight (Cellectis 3.47 on CLLS5, JPM 3.47, PYPL 3.67 on PYPL3, ANTO 3.80, ADBE 4.00, LIN 4.13); only HON (5.73) and CFR (5.20, never rerun) are higher. PayPal's third run scored below its second (3.87 -> 3.67); Cellectis needed four reruns to move from 2.33 to 3.47, through a P0 regression on the way. HON reached 6 only on its deterministic snapshot. XBRL headline numbers are reliable; business model, competition, catalysts, counter-thesis and sector KPIs are stale, generic or wrong in most reports.

**B. Can important financial numbers be trusted?**
Filed figures quoted with a citation: mostly yes (every FY2025 XBRL figure for ADBE, JPM and PYPL checked exact; CFR revenue, margins and segments PASS). Derived and headline aggregates: only in the deterministic snapshot, where three P0s (HON YoY sign, CFR x1000, JPM total debt 7.7x too low) are fixed and the layer now withholds rather than guesses. Two of those three are verified by replay, not by a live rerun of the affected company. Anything an LLM layer restates (council, red team, chair, synthesis) can carry the wrong period, scope or entity. Trust the cited filed figure; verify everything else.

**C. Can period/scope/entity handling be trusted?**
No. 89 of 323 independently checked claims (28%) are STALE, WRONG_PERIOD, WRONG_SCOPE or WRONG_ENTITY. On the four rerun companies (PYPL, ANTO, Cellectis, LIN) the share was 29% (23 of 78) on the first runs, 21% (18 of 84) on the day-2 reruns and 25% (21 of 85) at each company's latest run (PYPL3, ANTO2, CLLS5, LIN2): one in four checked claims is still stale or mis-attributed after every fix. New scope, period and entity errors appeared on reruns (ANTO2-1 mine capex as Group; LIN2-3 Iluka unit costs unattributed; the P0 exchange-as-Cellectis on CLLS3 and CLLS4, fixed live on CLLS5; forward target dates used as document periods on CLLS4 and CLLS5; IBA's equipment price as Cellectis industry economics on CLLS5).

**D. Is current-event research good enough?**
No. Catalysts/current scores at the latest run: ADBE 2, JPM 2, PYPL 2, CFR 3, CLLS 3, HON 5, ANTO 5, LIN 5. Missed: Cellectis's strategic pivot (on the first three runs; from CLLS4 the exit is stated but the .HEAL programmes, their data dates and the H2 2028 runway are not), Adobe's CEO succession, CFO exit, Semrush and DOJ settlement, PayPal's CEO change and litigation (still on PYPL3), JPM's Q3 results (selection fixed in code) and Apple Card transaction, CFR's Q1 FY27, Avolta disposal and Baume & Mercier sale, Lindian's Malawi export regime. PYPL2 stated an unconfirmed takeover rumour as fact. #314 (ADR-078) merged after that run; on PYPL3 the rumour is absent only because the claim was not regenerated, so the gate has never fired live.

**E. Is Discovery finding genuinely useful new names?**
Rarely. When the intent is read correctly (E2 after ADR-063) it returned 12 on-thesis names of 15, including useful small caps (Pensana, Rainbow, Lindian, ABX, Astron, ETM). Elsewhere: A found 1 name (Cellectis, already known), B 1 strict fit of 3, C nothing, D 2 strict fits of 10. Recall is the bottleneck: name-only mentions are dropped and the registries are mega-cap heavy. E3 (after the ranking fixes) kept 12 of 15 on thesis and returned 3 of 7 evidenced web names (E2: 1), but still ranked adjacent SCCO, CLF and FCX above Iluka and Meteoric and cut Lynas and Lindian.

**F. Is Discovery ranking them sensibly?**
Not as observed. Scores were constant per source and did not drive the order: Lynas ranked 14th with score 20, SCCO 3rd, and size-unknown mega caps outranked evidenced small caps. ADR-070 and ADR-073 fix the scorer input and apply relevance before the cap, but relevance is still the last sort key and size-unknown still passes as eligible. Live on E3 the fix is partial: web scores vary and the cut is named, but registry scores stay constant (85/75) and a verified size outranks fit, so 4 evidenced names were cut while two theme-unverified ones were returned.

**G. Which sectors work best?**
US filers with clean XBRL, for the financial snapshot only: industrials after the fixes (HON 6) and software and payments numbers (ADBE and PYPL figures exact, even on a 52/53-week year). ASX development-stage mining is the best non-US case on facts read straight from ASX documents (Lindian's JORC reserve and resource, licences, ownership and offtake terms PASS; latest-period 6 after ADR-076). Luxury PDFs give good segment and regional facts (CFR 6 of 10 claims PASS).

**H. Which sectors currently work poorly?**
Dual-listed non-US biotech (Cellectis: usefulness 1 -> 2 -> 1 -> 2 -> 3 over five runs; the interim is acquired but its figures do not reach the report, no current cash or runway, the new pipeline missing, a P0 wrong-registrant regression on two runs). UK-listed operating mining (Antofagasta: 2 -> 4; unclassified, financial core two periods stale). Banks (JPM 3: industrial semantics, no bank KPIs extracted, a subsidiary leak). Payments (PayPal 3 on all three runs: unclassified, no payments playbook, a P0 on the second run, a latest-quarter regression on the third). Software as research (no playbook, Tempus AI as a peer, dissolved segments). In Discovery: European biotech and small/mid payments (recall).

**I. What is the single biggest remaining generic weakness?**
The platform acquires the right primary documents and does not read them, then writes a research state that is not derived from what it holds. 114 of 175 audited gaps were DOCUMENT_ACQUIRED_EXTRACTION_FAILED; the issuer-document fact channel read 0 facts for HON (before), ADBE, JPM, PYPL, ANTO, Cellectis and LIN (only CFR, 53 facts, and HON after its fix, 10 facts, read any; Cellectis stayed at 0 on all five runs, including three with the H1 6-K/A held); and every one of the 16 claim-audited reports contradicts itself about what it holds. The final reruns add a sharper form of the same weakness: when a figure from a held filing finally reaches a statement, a mis-assigned period tag can discard it (CLLS5-1).

**J. What are the next THREE engineering priorities?**
1) P-1: period tagging: a forward target date in a document ('runway into H2 2028', 'data in H2 2027') is never the document's period; the period comes from the filing's report date, and the period-agreement guard does not discard a results figure because an event filing beside it names a future date (CLLS5-1, CLLS4-2). 2) P-2: one research state derived from the ledger: chair and tech counts, 'latest disclosure', question status and gaps computed from the findings and the document inventory, so the chair cannot say 0 findings against 32 (CLLS2-3) and a gap cannot deny what a finding states (PYPL3-1, CLLS2-8, ANTO2-3, LIN2-4). 3) P-3: non-US primary financial extraction: ifrs-full XBRL for 20-F filers, statement figures from 6-K interims (the H1 6-K/A held on three Cellectis runs, facts_read=0), and ASX Appendix 5B cash and runway. The claim gate (ADR-078 has never fired live) and US statement extraction follow as HIGH items.

## Appendix — evidence discrepancies and conventions

- campaign_state.md is the source of truth. Its run table lists the ADBE and JPM job ids (b67e3b11, 60e705ec) in the company-id column. The company ids are 4741fc77 (ADBE) and 3f8da8ae (JPM) per the rerun list and the day-1 draft; this report uses those.
- The defect log says 'score=None for every item' (D, E2). That is wrong: the score lives in items[].relevance_score_pre_scan (a helper read a non-existent 'score' key). The Discovery evaluation recomputed it exactly. Hypothesis J is confirmed on the stored values.
- E2 thesis fit is 12/15 (SCCO, CLF, FCX adjacent), per the detailed Discovery evaluation. An earlier note said about 13/15.
- E2 Tavily credits: the run payload stores 20. An earlier campaign note estimated 38. The stored value is used; the daily total of about 287 on 2026-11-01 is the orchestrator's count.
- The E2 cap cut ('6 of 7 theme-verified web names') is inferred from funnel counts; the payload never stored the cut names. ADR-073 now records them.
- PR #297 (ADR-062 s.5) also fixed the council-status header (H7), in addition to H1, H2 and H5. Not live-observed, because no later run had a failed agent.
- Hypothesis B is fixed only in the deterministic layer. Council, red-team, chair and editor period and scope swaps remain (JPM-6, PYPL2-5, ADBE FY margins beside Q3 growth).
- ADBE-2 is only partly fixed by #304. The contradiction badge is fixed; the 'secondary_web' source kind on a 10-K copy and 'not from a filing' on filed figures are not.
- C2's root fix was #298 (ADR-064 s.3, the narrow-gutter column interleave). #304 (ADR-071) is an independent guard that also stops the manufactured conflict.
- #309 (ADR-072, unclosed period) was shelved after 4 review rounds and left open as a draft with an explanatory comment. ADBE-3 stays open (P1).
- ADR-077 (#313, 342b660) and ADR-078 (#314, 48b5777) merged on 2026-11-02 after every day-2 scored run. The final reruns resolve the marker the 2026-11-02 version of this report left in section 14: ADR-077 is live at selection on CLLS3-CLLS5 (its figures are not extracted); ADR-078 has not fired in any live run (PYPL3 did not regenerate the claim), so PYPL2-1 is 'not reproduced live; fix proven by replay only'.
- Final-rerun identifiers: job and report ids are from the job list and the evaluations; research-run ids were not recorded for PYPL3, CLLS3, CLLS4 or CLLS5. PYPL3 and CLLS3 ran on 48b5777 (2026-11-02); CLLS4 on a8f8f8a and CLLS5 on 6df9d89 (2026-11-03).
- Status rule (applied to every defect in this version): a defect counts as fixed only when a live rerun shows it gone (FIXED_LIVE). A merged fix with replay or code evidence only is 'fix merged, not proven live'. A defect absent on a rerun without a targeted fix stays open (NOT_REPRODUCED). CLLS4-4 (Regenxbio) is gone on CLLS5 and the orchestrator's campaign summary calls it fixed; with no PR targeting it this report lists it as NOT_REPRODUCED.
- Register IDs PYPL3-x, CLLS3-x, CLLS4-x and CLLS5-x follow each evaluation's own numbering. Many carry an earlier ID (stated in the note column; for example PYPL3-2 carries PYPL-1/PYPL2-2). The register therefore counts some lineages once per run in which an evaluation logged them anew: the P0-P3 totals count register entries, not distinct root causes.
- FW-1 (the inert ADR-066 publisher firewall) was found in code while fixing CLLS3-1 (PR #315), not by a report evaluation. Its severity (P1) was assigned by the campaign orchestrator.
- Cellectis June 2026 cash in the held 6-K/A: the CLLS3 and CLLS5 evaluations and the orchestrator's campaign summary give $169m; the CLLS4 evaluation gives $166.8m (and H1 burn $29.7m, where CLLS3 gives -$42m). No report stated either figure, so no verdict depends on it; the discrepancy is not reconciled here.
- The Calyxt risk text (CLLS2-2): the day-2 evaluation places it in Cellectis's FY2022 20-F; the #317 review traced the CLLS2 case to Cellectis's own 2017 20-F (CIK 1627281) and classed it as staleness, not entity. Either way it is the issuer's own old filing. The register keeps CLLS2-2 under hypothesis D, A with that caveat.
- The orchestrator's campaign summary says that on CLLS5 no exchange text remains on any reader surface. The CLLS5 evaluation records two exchange-derived gap texts rendered in section 13 (CLLS5-6, P3). This report follows the evaluation: no exchange statement remains in findings, section leads, the synthesis or the chair; gap residue remains.
- Discovery E3: which 4 of the 8 beyond-cap names are web-admitted is a deduction from the funnel counts and the ranking key (most likely CRML, ARA, NB, PKX), not stored data; per-name state is not persisted for cut names (E3-8). The E3 evaluation used stored data plus two ASX announcement-page reads (MEI, KNI) to check the takeover and the market cap.
- ADBE evaluation inconsistencies: the CEO-succession 8-K is dated 2026-09-02 in the claims and 2026-09-08 in ADBE-7; the CFO 8-K 2026-06-08 vs 2026-06-11. Its 'playbook captures economics' opens with 'Largely yes' but its own explanation says the economics are not captured; this report reads it as 'No'.
- Severity: ADBE-3 (a forecast shown as a reported actual) is P1 in the evaluation but meets the spec's P0 wording ('unsupported claim presented as fact'). Each defect keeps its logged severity here.
- Status NOT_REPRODUCED marks defects absent on a rerun with no targeted fix (PYPL-5, PYPL2-5, ANTO-4, CLLS-12, CLLS4-4, LIN-1, LIN-6). They are not counted as fixed. CLLS-11 was NOT_REPRODUCED on day 2 and regressed on CLLS5 (CLLS5-3); it is open again. CLLS-3 is counted as fixed live because #311 removed its cause (no filings) and the chair declined, but the guard that would stop the same sentence elsewhere does not exist.
- HON-after's stored issuer-document state labels the annual 'FY2026' and the current period 'H1 2026', and withholds a revenue conflict built from an aerospace trade article, a Q1 10-Q figure and the Q2 release under one '2026' key. It fails closed (no value shown) but is a period-label defect on a US filer.
- The day-2 Cellectis and Lindian evaluations number findings differently from report.json (LIN2 uses investor-page labels F1..F53). Finding numbers in this report follow each evaluation.

## RESEARCH INTEGRITY CLOSURE — IN PROGRESS

Follow-on phase opened 2026-11-03. Goal: close the generic P0/P1 integrity defects above and rerun HON, LIN, Cellectis,
PYPL, JPM and CFR. **Acceptance bar** (not yet met):
- P0 = 0 and material P1 = 0 across all six.
- No false "not found" gap where the answer is in an acquired primary document.
- No wrong-company finding, no fabricated conflict, no search-count contradiction and no period-duration mismatch.
- At least 7/10 overall usefulness for ordinary operating companies.

### Corrective PRs (merged and deployed)

| PR | ADR | Change |
|---|---|---|
| #319 | 081 | One central outbound identity; no personal contact in any User-Agent |
| #320 | 083 | A passage's subject decides whose fact it is |
| #321 | 084 | Re-read held primary documents before searching the web or writing an absence gap; metric-bearing contracts |
| #322 | 082 | Ledger canonical state |
| #323 | 080 | Temporal semantics (forward dates are not period tags) |
| #324 | 085 | SEC iXBRL / IFRS facts |
| #325 | 089 | Peer similarity |
| #326 | 087 | ASX Appendix 5B; period supersession |
| #327 | 088 | Statement kind, business stage, customer funds, source kind (migration 045) |
| #328 | 086 | One gap-status vocabulary; council/chair claims held to what they cite |
| #329 | 090 | Statement slots hold only an ended period's issuer number; count-satisfied questions still re-read |
| #330 | 091 | Status lines from the canonical state; scope, fiscal calendar, phantom conflicts |

Deployed API/Web: `3203fc3`.

### Open P1 (generic)

- **False "not found" gaps (14–27 per report).** The re-read runs (23–29 queries per report), but:
  - some gap questions never enter it (JPM CET1);
  - most queries miss table facts;
  - gaps are not reconciled with the re-read;
  - the LIN annual reports were parsed only to page 40.
- **Search status.** Two producers report search counts, and planned searches are counted as executed. CFR3 ran 0 searches but showed 41.
- **Chair conflicts.** The chair narrates conflicts between findings that agree: 6 in HON4, 18 in CLLS7.
- **Stale periods.** FY2025 facts partially close FY2026 gaps (PYPL, HON).
- **Segment and duration.** Segment figures are stated as Firm, and nine-month figures are labelled Q3 (JPM).
- **Corporate action.** HON is still described with its pre-spin-off (June 2026) perimeter.
- **Liquidity.** The Cellectis runway ignores US$144.8M of term deposits.
- **Wrong-company items.** They persist in "current developments": HONA under HON, plus unrelated items for LIN, CLLS and PYPL.
